## Tasks

### Phase 1 — Foundations, types, providers, custody
- [x] 🟩 1.0: Capture both clinics' real Cliniko treatment-note template section lists, and confirm whether they are identical. **Precedes the schema constant** — the canonical set is only defensible as a superset once both real templates have been seen.

  **Template A — captured 2026-08-04** (three groups, six text fields, one checkbox):

  | Group | Field | Type |
  |---|---|---|
  | History | Presenting complaint/patient progress | rich text (HTML) |
  | Examination | Assessment | plain text |
  | Examination | Informed Consent | **checkbox — never written by this app** (see Critical Constraints) |
  | Examination | Diagnosis | plain text |
  | Treatment/Management | Treatment | plain text |
  | Treatment/Management | Response to treatment | plain text |
  | Treatment/Management | Management/Advice | plain text |

  Three findings this capture forces, all to be resolved when the second template arrives:
  - **The canonical schema is missing interval history.** "Presenting complaint/**patient progress**" carries progress-since-last-appointment, which is distinct from presenting complaint (canonical 1) and from response to treatment (canonical 10 — within-session). On a return-visit-heavy caseload this is the highest-volume field in the template, and nothing currently maps to it. Add a canonical section.
  - **The mapping model must express more than `{canonical_key: target_field}`.** Targets carry a group, a content type (HTML vs plain — never emit formatting into a plain field), and in one case a non-text type that is explicitly unmappable.
  - **Six-into-seventeen is heavily many-to-one.** Four canonical sections had no natural target; resolved 2026-08-04 by folding three into existing fields and marking `consent` intentionally unmapped (see `Schema / Data Changes`). The round-2 "Unmapped content" target therefore has no consumer under Template A — keep it, since a future practitioner's mapping will leave genuine gaps, but it is not exercised by this template.

  - **Confirmed 2026-08-04: both clinics use this same template, and Management/Advice is the last field.** So there is exactly ONE mapping profile today. The per-practitioner mapping layer stays — it is the commercial requirement, for future practitioners with different templates — but `template_profile_id` resolves to the sole profile automatically. Do NOT make the clinician choose a profile from a list of one; surface a chooser only when more than one exists.
  - **Done 2026-08-04.** The template is recorded, the canonical set is reconciled (17 sections with stable keys, `progress_since_last_visit` added, listed in `Schema / Data Changes`), every key carries its meaning, ownership and target, and the unmapped-content question is resolved. Carried to Task 3.1: practitioner confirmation of the two non-obvious mapping calls (`outcome_measures` → Assessment; `referrals_investigations` / `follow_up_review` → Management/Advice).
- [x] 🟩 1.1: Note types in `note.py` — `NoteAssertion`, `NoteSpan`, `NoteProposal`, `ConfirmationDecision`, `GeneratedSection`, `NoteWarning`, `GeneratedNote`, `NoteRequest`, `NoteModelProvider` Protocol, and the canonical 17-section constant (listed in `Schema / Data Changes`). Frozen pydantic, `extra="forbid"`, mirroring `TranscriptDocument` (`transcription.py:252-292`). Reuse `SESSION_ID_PATTERN`.
  - **`NoteAssertion` is the unit `GeneratedSection` holds** — not `NoteSpan`. A `transcript` assertion holds exactly one span with one contiguous `source_coords = (segment_index, first_word_index, last_word_index)`; the type makes multi-interval assembly unrepresentable rather than merely checked.
  - A non-`transcript` assertion carries `proposal_id`, `shown_text_digest`, `config_digest`, and a `ConfirmationDecision(decision, timestamp)`. `NoteProposal` is structurally distinct from `NoteAssertion`, so an unconfirmed proposal cannot be written by construction.
  - `GeneratedNote` additionally carries `session_id` (bound, `SESSION_ID_PATTERN`), `template_profile_id`, the confirmed cluster→role assignment, `transcript_digest`, and `config_digest`. **Define `transcript_digest` precisely**: algorithm, version tag, and exact byte domain — the decrypted canonical `TranscriptDocument.to_bytes()` output, not ciphertext — computed in one place and verified identically by `read_note` and `complete_session`.
  - Done when: types import cleanly, mypy strict passes, a round-trip test passes, and tests assert that a `NoteProposal` cannot be placed in a `GeneratedSection`, that a `transcript` assertion spanning two intervals is unrepresentable, and that a non-`transcript` assertion without a `ConfirmationDecision` is rejected at construction.
  - **Done 2026-08-04.** `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note.py`. All three structural rejections are pinned in `tests/test_note.py`. Decisions taken against the code, recorded so later phases inherit them rather than re-litigate:
    - `NoteAssertion.note_span` is ONE `NoteSpan`, and `SourceCoords` is a 3-field `NamedTuple` (not a model), so two intervals fail validation at construction — no list of spans, no list of coordinate triples.
    - Construction requires the confirmation record's PRESENCE, its `confirmed` value, and a matching `proposal_id`, but deliberately does NOT recompute `shown_text_digest` against the text: that verification is `write_note`'s (Task 6.2), and making it unrepresentable here would leave 6.2's defence-in-depth check untestable.
    - `role_unconfirmed` is likewise left to Check 3, not enforced by the type: a clinician-owned section populated with no confirmed role must stay REACHABLE so 5.3 can fire on it.
    - `transcript_digest` = `sha256-v1:<hex>` over the decrypted canonical `TranscriptDocument.to_bytes()`. `digest_bytes()` is the single primitive; `complete_session` digests the transcript plaintext it already holds, so nothing re-serialises.
    - `NoteWarning` carries `note_warning_code` + `severity` validated against `NOTE_WARNING_SEVERITY`, so a check cannot re-grade `mapping_drop` to error later. Codes registered now are Check 3's five; Tasks 5.1/5.2/5.4 EXTEND the registry.
    - `reconstruct_span_text()` is the canonical word-range→text rule (strip each word, join with single spaces) — Whisper's leading spaces would otherwise make "exact reconstruction" fail on whitespace alone in Task 5.1.
- [x] 🟩 1.2: `normalise_token()` / `content_tokens()` in `note.py` — the single normalisation source (see the shared-helpers inventory). Build on `_STRIP_PUNCT_RE` (`transcription.py:203`).
  - Done when: a test pins that autofill matching and checker tokenisation call the same function.
  - **Done 2026-08-04.** `note.py` imports `transcription._STRIP_PUNCT_RE` rather than restating the rule. `content_tokens` drops punctuation-only tokens and pure disfluencies but KEEPS negation and hedging — dropping "not"/"never" would make a negated claim and its opposite tokenise identically, defeating Check 2 before it is written.
  - The Done-when is pinned as far as today's code allows: 4.1 and 5.2 do not exist yet, so `test_normalisation_has_exactly_one_implementation` asserts by source scan that `def normalise_token` occurs exactly once in `src/` and `_STRIP_PUNCT_RE` has exactly one owner plus one consumer. **Tasks 4.1 and 5.2 must upgrade this to a call-site pin** once there are call sites.
- [x] 🟩 1.3: Both providers in `note.py` — `ExtractiveNoteProvider` (verbatim transcript spans, cue-matched to sections, role-aware once 2.2 lands) and `MockNoteModelProvider(behaviour=...)` with the Axis B behaviours. Ship in `src/`, following `MockSpeechProvider` (`speech.py:115`).
  - Done when: both satisfy the Protocol and `MockNoteModelProvider` produces each Axis B behaviour deterministically.
  - **Done 2026-08-04.** 13 Axis B behaviours (`MOCK_BEHAVIOURS`), each a pure function of the request. A behaviour whose failure class the fixture cannot express (no laterality / number / negation / name token) raises `NoteProviderError` instead of silently degrading into a different, quietly-passing class — that would let a Phase 5 cell go green on the wrong evidence.
  - `ExtractiveNoteProvider` routes whole utterances by cue into the first matching canonical section, so every assertion is one contiguous interval that reconstructs exactly. The Critical-Constraint role gate is enforced NOW (clinician-owned sections take only confirmed-clinician, non-interrogative utterances); 2.2 supplies the preselection heuristic behind `clinician_speaker`, not the gate.
  - Protocol conformance is proved statically inside `note.py` under `if TYPE_CHECKING`, because mypy is configured over `src` only — a test-side annotation would prove nothing.
  - Cue lists are a first cut authored against the fixture transcripts, not clinical evidence. Task 6.4's real-transcript run is what decides whether they are usable, and Task 9.1's shipping gate is what binds that judgment to copy enablement.
- [x] 🟩 1.4: Tripwire signatures in `logging_setup.py:57-87` — `note_sections`, `note_spans`, `note_warnings`, `span_text`, `note_excerpt`, `transcript_utterances`, quoted and unquoted. Test over `repr` and `model_dump_json` of every note model **including `NoteRequest`**.
  - Done when: each model's lone repr is dropped by the filter.
  - **Done 2026-08-04.** All six mandated names registered in quoted and unquoted form, plus four more the audit forced: `note_assertions`, `note_span` (singular — the actual `NoteAssertion` field; `note_spans=` would not match it), `note_warning_code`, and `note_confirmation`. Without those, a lone `GeneratedSection`, `NoteAssertion`, `NoteWarning` or `ConfirmationDecision` repr — and their JSON, which no class-name signature could catch — passed the filter. `NoteUtterance` needs nothing new: it holds `TranscriptWord`s under the already-registered `transcript_words`.
  - Coverage keys on FIELD NAMES, so an empty `GeneratedSection` is dropped too; the test asserts exactly `2 x len(models)` drops over repr and JSON of all nine note models.
  - **Amended 2026-08-04 (peer round 3, PR-HIGH-001, operator-approved scope expansion beyond this task's declared `:57-87`):** registering signatures is only half a tripwire — the filter has to SEE the text. `PayloadTripwireFilter` scanned `record.getMessage()` alone, and handler filters run before the formatter appends `exc_info`/`stack_info`, so a traceback could carry these very signatures past it. The scan surface now covers message + exception + stack. See Round 3's `/fix notes`.
- [x] 🟩 1.5: `NOTE_FILENAME` and the `complete_session` custody fix in `session_store.py`. If `note.enc` exists: fsync + verify-decrypt + parse + session-binding + `transcript_digest` match, all before `delete_session_key`. **Fails closed, symmetric with the transcript** — any failure retains the key and Complete does not proceed. Retaining custody is precisely what keeps regeneration possible; deleting the note and completing would delete the key too, making the transcript unreadable and regeneration impossible.
  - The clinician's exits are **Regenerate note** or an explicit, confirmed choice to delete the note and complete without one — never a silent deletion.
  - Done when: the 56-function custody suite passes unchanged, plus tests for note-absent, note-valid, note-corrupt-ciphertext, note-authenticated-but-malformed, note-wrong-session, note-digest-mismatch, and unlink-failure — each asserting the key is retained.
  - **Done 2026-08-04.** All seven cases plus two the case list did not name: audio-header-vs-directory binding precedence, and unresolvable session identity. Existing custody tests pass unchanged.
    - Two helpers landed beside it: `_resolve_session_identity()` (audio header authoritative, directory name as fallback, **fail closed** when neither yields a well-formed id) and `_verify_note_for_completion()`.
    - `complete_session` gained `delete_note: bool = False` — the clinician's explicit "complete without a note" exit. It unlinks FIRST and a failed unlink aborts with the key retained, so Complete can never proceed over a note still on disk. Default False, so every existing caller is unchanged.
    - `session_store` now imports `note` at CALL time inside `_verify_note_for_completion`. Not a cycle: `note.py` imports `session_store` at module load for `SESSION_ID_PATTERN`/`atomic_write_bytes`, so the dependency is one-way at import time by construction.

### Phase 2 — Speaker roles and diarization measurement
- [x] 🟩 2.1: Per-segment cepstral mean normalisation in `_segment_embedding` (`transcription.py:507-543`), to remove the gain nuisance that makes one loud and one quiet speaker separable as two clusters.
  - Done when: existing diarization tests pass and a new test shows a gain-shifted copy of a segment clusters with its original.
  - **Done 2026-08-04.** One line of behaviour (`log_mel = log_mel - log_mel.mean()`) plus the reasoning that justifies it. A gain `g` scales every power by `g²`, so it adds the SAME constant `2·ln(g)` to all 24 log-mel bands; subtracting the segment's mean across bands removes that constant exactly. Equivalent to per-frame removal followed by the time average (the means commute), so it is applied to the already-averaged vector.
  - **Only the mel block is normalised.** The centroid is a power RATIO and already gain-invariant — the pre-fix run measured its gain sensitivity at `1.7e-4` against `4.61` per mel band. Folding it into the mean would mix two units and reintroduce a level dependence.
  - **Pure tones cannot test this and the first attempt at the fixture was wrong.** A tone's band energies are knife-edge functions of where it falls in the triangular filterbank, so a frequency change swamps any level change: a 220 Hz/2600 Hz fixture passed BEFORE the fix and proved nothing. The suite now uses `comb_pcm` (test helper) — a harmonic comb with `1/k**rolloff` decay, which puts real energy in every mel band so a level change moves every band equally, while `rolloff` varies spectral tilt the way two speakers do. `fundamental` must divide `SAMPLE_RATE` so one period tiles (exact and fast).
  - **All three new tests were run against the pre-fix code and all three failed**, with the predicted signature: a uniform `4.6086…4.6100` offset in every mel band, and the 2×2 loud/quiet × two-voice fixture clustering by LEVEL (`[s1, s2, s2, s1]`) instead of by voice. Tests: `test_gain_shift_leaves_the_embedding_unchanged` (feature-level, pins the mechanism), `test_a_gain_shifted_copy_clusters_with_its_original` (the Done-when), `test_two_voices_stay_separable_across_a_level_difference` (guards the downside — normalising away loudness must not normalise away spectral shape).
  - Both call sites are covered without change: `_segment_embedding` is the single feature function used by `label_speakers` and by the inline windowed path in `transcribe_session:901`, so the paired degenerate-case policy at `:907` is untouched.
- [x] 🟩 2.2: `speaker_role()` — **preselect** which cluster is the clinician from talk-time share, question-asking rate, and first-speaker order. The result is a default for the mandatory confirmation in Task 7.5, never an authority. The clinician-owned sections (`assessment`, `diagnosis`, `advice_home_exercise`, `management_plan`) populate only from confirmed-clinician utterances; unresolved or merged clustering leaves them blank with candidate quotations surfaced instead.
  - A clinician's *question* is not a diagnosis: cue matching into clinician-owned sections must exclude interrogative forms.
  - Done when: role preselection is a pure function with fixture tests; a test asserts clinician-owned sections stay empty when the role is unconfirmed; and a test asserts an interrogative clinician utterance does not populate `diagnosis`.
  - **Done 2026-08-04.** `note.speaker_role()` — pure, 13 fixture tests. **Two of the three Done-when clauses were already met by Phase 1** and are pinned by tests that predate this task: `test_clinician_owned_sections_stay_blank_without_a_confirmed_role` and `test_a_clinician_question_is_not_a_diagnosis` (with `test_clinician_imperative_advice_reaches_the_note` guarding the round-1 MED-001 direction). This task added the preselector and the end-to-end link between them.
  - **It lives in `note.py`, not `transcription.py`, and that is forced rather than chosen.** The question signal needs `is_interrogative`, and `note.py` already imports `transcription`; putting the function the other way round would be an import cycle. It sits directly above `ExtractiveNoteProvider`, next to the routing rule it feeds.
  - **What stops a preselection becoming an authority is the RETURN TYPE.** `speaker_role()` returns `SpeakerRolePreselection`, not the `str` that `clinician_speaker` accepts, so reaching the label costs an explicit `.preselected_clinician_speaker` — visible at the call site and not skippable under `mypy strict`. Nothing in the pipeline calls it yet; Task 7.5 owns the control that converts it into a confirmed role.
  - **Weights, and their honest status:** question rate 0.60, first speaker 0.25, talk-time share 0.15. They are authored against consultation structure, NOT measured — **Task 2.3 is what confirms or reverses them**. The talk-time term is the only one whose DIRECTION is an assumption (history-taking means the patient talks more; explanation and exercise instruction mean the clinician does), which is why it is weighted to break near-ties rather than to decide. `test_question_rate_outranks_both_talk_time_and_speaking_first` pins that ordering: a speaker holding >80% of talk time AND the first-speaker bonus still loses to a consistent question-asker.
  - **The question rate is additively smoothed** (`questions / (utterances + 1)`): on the raw rate a speaker with one interrogative sentence scores 1.0 and outranks a clinician who asked six questions in nine turns. `test_a_lone_question_does_not_outrank_a_sustained_asker` builds exactly that case — and uses a third speaker label, which also shows the function does not assume exactly two clusters.
  - **No preselection at all** for an empty transcript, for a MERGED single-label clustering (no second cluster to choose against — it must not fall back to "the only speaker"), or for a tie between the top two. `test_an_unresolved_preselection_leaves_clinician_owned_sections_blank` carries that through to the note: merged clustering → `None` → every clinician-owned section empty.
  - **The tie rule uses a `1e-9` float-noise epsilon, not a confidence threshold.** Genuinely tied clusters can miss exact equality by an ulp once weights are summed. There is deliberately no "too close to call" threshold above it: what that should be is a question about measured accuracy, i.e. Task 2.3's. `margin` is returned so Task 7.5 can present a weak preselection as weak; this function applies no threshold to it.
  - **Segments that transcribed to no text are excluded from BOTH signals**, so the two rates are measured over one population — counting a wordless turn as a turn would silently depress that speaker's question rate.
  - **`SpeakerEvidence` carries counts and seconds only, never transcript text**, because a role preselection is exactly what a UI renders and a diagnostic logs (Critical Constraint: no clinical content in logs). `test_carries_no_transcript_text` asserts it through `repr()`. Candidate quotations for Task 7.5 are deliberately NOT here — that screen holds the transcript and can quote from it directly.
- [x] 🟩 2.3a: **Task 2.3 measurement harness** — `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/speaker_eval.py` (new; CLI `main()` in-module, the `benchmark.main` shape), a thin `scripts/measure-speakers.py` launcher, a `cepstral_mean_normalisation: bool = True` keyword threaded `label_speakers` → `_segment_embedding` (`transcription.py:617`, `:508`; the inline call in `transcribe_session` keeps the default, so shipped behaviour is unchanged), `desktop/tests/test_speaker_eval.py`, and a `scripts/README.md` line. (Added 2026-09-03 from the handoff's agent-doable prep (a); practitioner-decided the same day: Audacity label tracks, 16 kHz mono WAV only, placed here before 2.3; hardened by a scoped `/review-plan`, which is why the spec below is exact. It turns Task 2.3 into a RUN once recordings exist.)
  - **Input contract.** One directory. Each recording is `<name>.wav` — 16 kHz, mono, 16-bit PCM (the pipeline's own `SAMPLE_RATE`), read with the standard-library `wave` module; any other rate, channel count or width is REFUSED per file with a message naming the required format and the Audacity conversion (Project Rate 16000 → Tracks ▸ Mix ▸ Mix Stereo Down to Mono → Export Audio ▸ WAV 16-bit PCM). No resampling. Beside it, `<name>.txt` — an Audacity exported label track: one `start<TAB>end<TAB>label` line per labelled stretch (seconds); lines beginning with `\` (Audacity's spectral-selection rows) are ignored; a point label (`start == end`) is ignored and counted. Labels are ROLE names (`clinician`, `patient`, `parent`…), never real names; exactly one distinct label must equal `clinician` (case-insensitive after strip) or the file is refused, naming the labels found. Overlapping or gapped label spans are data, not errors. A WAV without a `.txt`, or vice versa, is reported and skipped.
  - **Measurement path** (Design Decision: measure the shipped pipeline). Per recording: `tempfile.mkdtemp` → a fresh 32-hex session id directory → `wrap_key_to_file` on a new in-memory `SessionCrypto` → the WAV's PCM through `SessionChunkStore.create` / `append_chunk` / `finish` (the `_make_store` shape with the REAL key blob) → `transcribe_session(session_dir, crypto, provider, frame_probability)` unchanged; its labels ARE the "after 2.1" condition. `discard_session(session_dir, crypto)` runs in a `finally`, so the harness never leaves a copy of any recording or transcript (the retention decision for the practitioner's WAVs themselves stays open and theirs). "Before 2.1": slice each VAD segment's PCM from the WAV bytes with `extract_segment_pcm`'s floor arithmetic (the identity `TestWindowPcmSliceIdentity` pins) and call `label_speakers(pcms, cepstral_mean_normalisation=False)`. Self-check: `label_speakers(pcms)` with the default MUST equal the document's labels, else that recording is reported as a HARNESS fault (both sites mirror one degenerate policy, round 42 LOW-011); a pipeline result that is all `speaker_1` over two or more segments is reported "merged" and not scored.
  - **Alignment rule** (the residue the task wording leaves implicit). A VAD segment's ground truth is the label with the largest overlap duration against `[start, end)`; zero overlap → UNLABELLED, excluded from every metric but counted; majority label covering under 80 % of the segment's overlapped duration → MIXED, still scored by majority but counted. Both counts print per recording because they bound the achievable accuracy.
  - **Metrics**, per recording and per condition, all label-set agnostic so a D-S1 option (a) build reuses them for k > 2: (1) cluster accuracy — the best INJECTIVE mapping from predicted to true labels, duration-weighted share of labelled segments correctly assigned, plus the unweighted count; (2) the seconds confusion matrix predicted × true, which is where a third voice's merge becomes visible; (3) per-cluster purity; (4) predicted vs true speaker count; (5) role — `speaker_role(document)` (for "before", over a copy of the document carrying the re-clustered labels) is CORRECT when the preselected cluster's majority true speaker is `clinician` (Design Decision), WRONG otherwise, NONE when no preselection; record `margin` and the clinician's `talk_time_share`, since Task 2.2 flagged the talk-time DIRECTION for 2.3 to confirm or reverse. Aggregate: mean cluster accuracy and role CORRECT / WRONG / NONE counts across recordings, per condition.
  - **Output.** A Markdown table on stdout ready to paste into Task 2.3, headed by the resolved whisper model name. NO transcript text is printed or logged, ever — the module logs nothing, progress lines name files only, and `SpeakerEvidence` is text-free by design.
  - **CLI.** `apply_offline_env()` then `assert_offline_env()` first (the `benchmark.main` order), then `SileroVad()` and `WhisperSpeechProvider(model_name=resolve_whisper_model())`, whose existing "run scripts/setup-models.py" errors cover absent models. Run by the PRACTITIONER from a normal terminal (`docs/lessons.md`: agent shells cannot see the user's model cache): `.venv\Scripts\python.exe scripts\measure-speakers.py <recordings-dir>`.
  - **Structure.** `evaluate_recording(wav_path, labels_path, provider, frame_probability) -> RecordingResult` takes the provider and VAD backend as parameters so the whole path is CI-testable with `MockSpeechProvider` plus an amplitude VAD; only `main`'s model wiring is real-ML. Pure helpers with their own tests: `parse_audacity_labels`, `read_wav_pcm`, `align_segments`, `cluster_metrics`, `role_outcome`, `render_report`.
  - **Tests** (`tests/test_speaker_eval.py`, CI-safe unless noted). Label parsing (tab format, `\` rows, point labels, missing and duplicate `clinician`, case); WAV refusal (stereo, 44.1 kHz, 24-bit) carrying the conversion message; alignment (majority, unlabelled, the 80 % boundary); metrics on hand-built label sets, including a three-true/two-predicted case whose confusion matrix shows the merge and whose accuracy is bounded by the third speaker's share; role CORRECT / WRONG / NONE; a report built from a document holding a sentinel word must not contain it; numpy-gated: `label_speakers(..., cepstral_mean_normalisation=False)` SPLITS the gain-shifted `comb_pcm` pair that `test_a_gain_shifted_copy_clusters_with_its_original` proves clusters together with it on — the pin that the toggle is the real pre-2.1 condition; end-to-end `evaluate_recording` over a synthetic two-tone WAV and a generated label file with `MockSpeechProvider` and the amplitude VAD, asserting the after-condition labels equal the pipeline's and that the temporary directory is gone afterwards, including when the provider raises.
  - Done when: `ruff check .`, `mypy` (strict; the module is under `packages`) and `pytest` are clean in `desktop/`, and the practitioner can point the script at a folder and receive the table. Verification: the tests above, and the `Validation / Verification` harness line.
  - Excluded, revisit only if needed: a VAD-only mode without Whisper (role needs transcribed text, so it would measure clustering alone); resampling (decided 2026-09-03).
  - **Done (2026-09-04, `/execute-loop` run=stage-2, executor `claude-fable-5-1`; composer-verified ruff clean, mypy strict clean across 30 files, pytest 1267 → 1329).** Landed: `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/speaker_eval.py` (pure `parse_audacity_labels` / `read_wav_pcm` / `align_segments` / `cluster_metrics` / `role_outcome` / `render_report`, the ML-free `score_document` seam, `evaluate_recording`, `find_recording_pairs`, `main`); `transcription.py` gained `cepstral_mean_normalisation: bool = True` on `label_speakers` → `_segment_embedding` (the inline `transcribe_session` call untouched, so shipped behaviour is unchanged); `scripts/measure-speakers.py` (thin launcher); a `scripts/README.md` line; `desktop/tests/test_speaker_eval.py` (+62 test items — 3 Windows-only end-to-end through the real DPAPI-wrapped temporary store, 3 numpy-gated toggle pins, the rest CI-safe).
  - **Decisions taken inside the spec:** (1) label case-variants of ANY role are refused with the spellings named, not only `clinician`'s — a silent two-speaker split is a wrong measurement with no signal, a refusal is fixed in seconds; (2) a preselected cluster with no labelled segment scores WRONG per the spec's "otherwise", its majority shown as `-` so the reader sees the labels, not the preselection, are what is missing; (3) the "before" role runs `speaker_role` over a `model_copy` of the pipeline's document carrying the re-clustered labels; (4) on a failure path the pipeline's suspended decrypt-stream generators are released via `traceback.clear_frames` BEFORE the store removal — on Windows their open handle on `audio.enc` would otherwise survive inside the traceback and defeat the removal the test pins (key deletion first is unaffected either way).
  - **Honest residues, stated in the module docstring:** the "before" condition holds every VAD segment's PCM in memory at once (the WAV is already plaintext on the practitioner's disk, so nothing widens at rest); the report echoes the recording's file stem; the teardown's four legs (key unlink, in-memory key destruction, session-directory and temp-root removal) are each attempted whatever the earlier legs did (a `finally` chain, round 64 PR-MED-008), the guard begins the moment the in-memory key exists, and the residue probe fails closed - only a positive `FileNotFoundError` counts as gone; destruction of the in-memory key is attempted on every exit and a failed attempt surfaced; teardown returns normally only when the root is positively gone, the key is destroyed and no leg failed - otherwise a fault names the root (or states none was created), the key state and every leg failure the OS reports, even when a later leg recovered the state, chained to whatever was in flight; a path already gone is not a failure and a failing removal stops at its first error, leaving the rest to the later legs and the probe (rounds 63-66) - what the harness cannot do is delete or inspect what the OS refuses, and that residue is named for by-hand removal, never hidden.

### Phase 4 — Autofill and prefill
- [x] 🟩 4.0: **Atomic-assertion authoring in the config schema.** Autofill expansions and prefill seeds are authored as explicit LISTS of atomic assertions in the config file — never as prose that gets split at runtime. Runtime decomposition of clinical prose would itself be an unverified inference, and it is the one place where "per assertion" could silently become "per blob". `note_config.py`'s validator rejects a non-list expansion.
  - Done when: a config declaring a multi-claim expansion as a single string fails validation with a message naming the fix.
  - Done 2026-08-10 (executor `stage-4`): `_refuse_prose_blob` + `mode="before"` field validators on `AutofillRule.expansion` AND `PrefillTemplate.seed_assertions` — the message names the fix for a clinician reader ("single string" / "JSON list" / "one entry per claim", with a concrete example) and names the forbidden alternative ("never splits prose"). Pinned at model level and END-TO-END through `load_note_config` (the message survives into `NoteConfigInvalidError`, tagged with the filename to edit). Small in-scope schema addition, same class as the existing trigger check: a `region_keywords` entry with no content tokens "could never fire" and is now rejected at construction (logged as an auto-disposed LOW fold-in — it guards the same authoring surface Task 4.0 owns). Suites 886 → 889. **Amended by Round 15 PR-HIGH-001 (2026-08-10, practitioner-disposed fix):** outer list shape alone was NOT proof of atomicity — entry-level guards now also enforce it: a lexical compound-entry check (`[.!?;]` + whitespace + more text; trailing terminators and decimals pinned accepted) with an explicit per-entry `single_claim` override (`SingleClaimEntry` for expansions, a flag on seeds — the author states it, no parser decides), and normalised duplicate rejection (expansion entries; `(section, seed_text)` pairs). What is enforced is exactly that and no more — conjunction-joined compounds and a falsely-applied override remain authoring-time human error outside any mechanical check, stated in the code. Suites 913 → 931. **Amended again by Round 16 PR-MED-001 (2026-08-11, practitioner-disposed Option B): the round-15 DETECTOR description above is SUPERSEDED** — a no-space semicolon join leaked through it (third separator form of the family), so the deny-list was replaced by the `_is_atomic_shape` ALLOW-LIST: an entry is accepted only when every character matches the conservative single-claim shape (documented exhaustively in `note_config.py`), and any unforeseen separator fails closed by construction. Override and duplicate semantics unchanged. Enforced surface and residue restated in code: the surviving compounds are comma-splice/conjunction joins inside the allowed alphabet, plus override misuse. Suites 931 → 949. **Amended a third time by Round 17 PR-MED-001 (2026-08-11, fix-induced, auto-routed):** the reframe's chain-continuation allowance was itself a leak (`"q.i.d. ice applied."` — continuation-vs-new-claim is semantic), closed by strict narrowing: letter-dot chains are TERMINAL-only, any text after the chain-final dot refuses into the override. The residue is now a DERIVED list, not prose (three falsifications in three rounds): comma/conjunction joins, hyphen-between-alphanumerics joins, digit-boundary joins — each mechanically inseparable from legitimate `mid-back`/`X-ray`/`1.5mg` wording — plus override misuse; accepted by design with Phase 7 confirmation and Phase 5 checking as compensating controls. Suites 949 → 952. **Amended a FOURTH time by Round 18 PR-MED-001 (2026-08-11, docs-only):** even the round-17 "derived" list was an enumeration, and it omitted the unrestricted in-claim punctuation category (`/`, `+`, `&`, parentheses, quotes, operators — all representable joins). The residue statement is now COMPLETE-BY-CONSTRUCTION: defined by REFERENCE to `_ALLOWED_IN_CLAIM_PUNCT` and the shape's permissive branches (the set is the source of truth, prose gives representative examples only), with the doc↔code sync pinned by two mechanical tests (every set member accepted mid-claim in both models; the by-reference markers asserted in both modules' source). Code unchanged — every member is accepted by design because legitimate clinical wording needs it. Suites 952 → 954.
- [x] 🟩 4.1: `note_fill.py` autofill — match trigger phrases against `TranscriptWord` sequences via `normalise_token`, emitting one **`NoteProposal` per atomic assertion** in the matched rule's expansion list, each carrying the rule id, the trigger's start time, and its exact text. Never an assertion, never one proposal for a multi-claim expansion.
  - Done when: a rule whose trigger is absent cannot fire; a three-assertion expansion yields three proposals; and a test asserts that a trigger spoken by the **patient** still only produces proposals, never an insertion — the round-1 CRIT's failure case, pinned.
  - Done 2026-08-10 (executor `stage-4`): `note_fill.py` `autofill_proposals(document, config)`. All three Done-when pins hold (`TestAutofill`), including the round-1 CRIT case pinned two ways: output type (all `NoteProposal`, none `NoteAssertion`) AND structurally (an emitted proposal placed in a `GeneratedSection` raises). Decisions recorded in the module docstring, each deliberate: (1) tokenisation is `content_tokens` applied PER WORD — the single source, so fillers inside a spoken trigger don't defeat the match; (2) matching is contiguous WITHIN ONE SEGMENT — a trigger straddling a VAD boundary fails toward silence, never toward a wrong candidate; (3) a rule fires on its FIRST occurrence and carries that start time — repeats add no evidence, only confirmation fatigue; (4) both emitters CANONICALISE the incoming config (`_canonical_config`, the round-12 boundary lesson) so the stamped `config_digest` derives from re-validated field data — forged/`model_construct` configs die typed, pinned. Proposal ids are content-derived sha256 (`autofill-<24hex>`), deterministic and inside the id grammar regardless of authored id length. The Phase 1 carried item is PAID for 4.1's half: `TestTokenisationCallSite` pins by monkeypatch that matching (autofill and prefill both) actually CALLS `note.content_tokens` — the source scan in `test_note.py` still stands, unchanged, as the no-second-implementation pin.

Selection mirrors `bind_template_profile`: explicit id is the clinician override and WINS OUTRIGHT — it need not have been detected (keywords are a heuristic, the clinician is not; with no match `trigger_start_seconds` is None), unknown id raises `UnknownPrefillError`; with no explicit choice, one detection selects itself, zero emit `()` (a region-free consult is normal, not an error), several raise `PrefillSelectionAmbiguousError` carrying the candidate ids — the chooser case; the engine never guesses the region. Both Done-when pins hold (`TestPrefillProposals`, `TestProposalsCannotBecomeAssertionsUnconfirmed`): a 3-seed template yields 3 proposals each carrying its own section, and an emitted proposal can neither enter a `GeneratedSection` nor become a `NoteAssertion` without a `ConfirmationDecision`. Suites 889 → 913 (ruff clean, mypy strict 27 files, 0 warnings).

### Phase 5 — Checking stage and the adversarial matrix
- [x] 🟩 5.0: **Make every `MockNoteModelProvider` behaviour prove it produced its NAMED Axis-B class.** Carried from Phase 1 peer round 6 (PR-MED-001), deferred here by operator decision 2026-08-04 — the fix is test-only and its correct specification depends on the Axis-B behaviour set THIS phase defines, so writing it in Phase 1 would have been a fourth guess. **Do this while defining the Axis B behaviours, not after.** Everything needed is recorded here; do not re-derive it.
  - **The invariant wanted:** every non-`faithful` behaviour either produces its SPECIFICALLY NAMED failure class, or raises `NoteProviderError` because the fixture cannot express that class. Output that merely differs from `faithful` is necessary but NOT sufficient. Results must also be structurally valid (re-validate through fresh pydantic construction) unless the requested class is `malformed_output`.
  - **Why the existing single-exit fingerprint is insufficient:** `MockNoteModelProvider._fingerprint` compares (section_key, content tokens, source_coords) against the faithful result and raises when they match. That proves DIFFERENCE, not CLASS — a cell can return a different result for the wrong reason, exercise the wrong checker or none, and still look like valid evidence that the named class was generated.
  - **Two concrete peer probes, reproduced against the current code — use them as the first two regressions:**
    - `obeys_injection` returned a management-plan assertion for an ordinary clinician examination containing NO injected instruction. Different from faithful, wrong class.
    - `negation_flip` returned `span_text=''`, which fails fresh `NoteSpan` validation. Different from faithful, structurally invalid.
  - **The fingerprint omits `speaker`.** A future speaker-attribution behaviour (misattributing an utterance to the wrong cluster — a real Axis-B class for this phase) would be wrongly REJECTED as indistinguishable from faithful. Either project `speaker` into the generic fingerprint or register such behaviours with their own oracle.
  - **A correction to the Phase-1 executor's reasoning that matters for how postconditions are written.** Phase 1 argued that an `invented_*` behaviour whose fixed text duplicates transcript content still produces its class "because it lands in a clinician-owned section". The peer showed that is not what preserves the class: what actually does is COORDINATE MISMATCH — the fixed multiword assertion cites only the first transcript word, so exact reconstruction fails. A hand-authored one-word `TranscriptWord` holding the whole phrase would defeat that reasoning entirely. **Do not rely on section ownership as the proof; write a behaviour-specific postcondition asserting the class directly.** That is the sound form for all of them.
  - **This class has now recurred in rounds 1, 4, 5 and 6** (laterality case-sensitivity → zero-doses and `Wilson` → punctuation/case cosmetics → wrong-class/invalid output). Every previous fix closed the named instances and left the class open. A per-behaviour postcondition asserted by an independent oracle is the shape that ends it; a generic difference check is the shape that has not.

### Phase 6 — Pipeline and controller plumbing
- [x] 🟩 6.1: The two-stage pipeline in `note.py`, matching Flow 1's ordering exactly. `compose_draft()` takes a resolved provider plus the config-resolved rules and returns the base note **plus proposals**, running no checks. `finalise_note()` takes the confirmed proposals **with their `ConfirmationDecision` records**, composes assertions, then runs **every** check and assembles `GeneratedNote`. Checks never run before confirmation, and confirmation evidence rides the artifact rather than the call.
  - Done when: `finalise_note()` refuses a note containing an unconfirmed proposal; refuses a non-`transcript` assertion whose `shown_text_digest` does not match the text actually confirmed; and confirmed clinician-authored assertions pass through Checks 2 and 3.
  - **Done (2026-08-11, executor `stage-6`).** `compose_draft(document, config, provider, template_profile_id=None, *, clinician_speaker=None, prefill_id=None) -> NoteDraft` and `finalise_note(draft, resolutions, document, config, *, created_at=None) -> GeneratedNote` in `note.py`, plus three new types: `NoteDraft` (the UNCHECKED stage-one hand-off — in-memory only, never persisted; inherits the structural guarantees from its member types, so it cannot smuggle unconfirmed content), `ProposalResolution` (the Flow 1 confirmation-evidence bundle exactly as the plan words it: the `shown_text_digest` of what the review surface actually RENDERED — deliberately not copied from the proposal — plus the `ConfirmationDecision`), and typed errors `NotePipelineError`/`ProposalEvidenceError`. Decisions recorded in code, each pinned: (1) compose runs NO checks and finalise calls `check_note` exactly once — pinned by monkeypatch call-count, not prose. (2) An UNRESOLVED proposal is not an exception: it flows into `check_note(pending_proposals=...)` and comes back as an `unconfirmed_proposal` ERROR riding the artifact — the property-4 ACTION-STATE refusal (`blocking_warnings()` non-empty, `write_note` refuses), matching the Phase 5 handoff's stated contract for that parameter. (3) A resolution — confirmed OR declined, widened decision-agnostic in round 25 LOW-001 — whose `shown_text_digest` is not the digest of the proposal's exact insertable text raises `ProposalEvidenceError` (a mis-rendered decline must not silently resolve a proposal the clinician never saw); so do a resolution naming a proposal the draft never emitted and two resolutions for one proposal. A declined proposal composes nothing and IS resolved (the type also pins: a declined decision cannot construct an assertion). (4) A stale document/config dies in `check_note`'s digest gate — deliberately not re-verified in finalise (one gate, one owner). (5) Section merge is deterministic: canonical order, provider assertions first, confirmed proposals after in draft order; composed assertions reuse the proposal id as assertion id (content-derived, collision-free with provider ids). (6) `compose_draft` constructs its request ONLY through `note_config.build_note_request` (call-time import; the AST reference-confinement guard is untouched — no new runtime `NoteRequest` reference exists, exact node counts unchanged) and its `clinician_speaker` must be the CONFIRMED role per the Task 7.5 guard. Both new models joined the Task 1.4 tripwire coverage list. Confirmed clinician-authored assertions pass Checks 2 AND 3: the fully-confirmed pipeline note's only warnings are the unsuppressible `clinician_asserted` reviews (pinned). Tests in NEW `desktop/tests/test_note_pipeline.py`.

  - **`write_note` enforces the invariants itself**, as defense in depth rather than trusting the UI: it refuses on any unresolved `error`, and it verifies every non-`transcript` assertion against its `ConfirmationDecision` and `shown_text_digest`, refusing an unbacked one.
  - `read_note` verifies `session_id` binding and `transcript_digest` using the single definition from Task 1.1 — the same code path `complete_session` uses.
  - **Done (2026-08-11, executor `stage-6`).** `write_note(session_dir, crypto, note, config)` / `read_note(session_dir, crypto)` in `session_store.py`, both over `atomic_write_bytes`, no AAD (the `write_transcript` docstring's recorded reason: `complete_session` verifies with a plain decrypt). The verification CORE is now the shared `_verified_note` (decrypt → parse → session binding → transcript-digest match): `_verify_note_for_completion` was refactored onto it and `read_note` calls it — the "same code path" Done-when is pinned STRUCTURALLY by a monkeypatch spy counting both callers, not by prose. `write_note`'s unbacked-assertion enforcement follows the no-content-escapes lesson — it confines the CLASS, not a field list: the note is first CANONICALISED (`_canonical_note`, the round-12 `_canonical_config` boundary shape: base-class serializer over field data + full re-validation), so every construction invariant (confirmation presence, confirmed-not-declined, proposal-id match) is re-established at once and a `model_construct`/subclass forgery dies typed (pinned); then the two relations construction deliberately does NOT verify are checked explicitly — `shown_text_digest` must be the digest of the assertion's OWN text, and each assertion's `config_digest` must equal the note's (evidence under a different config backs a different proposal; real coverage for prefill, whose staleness Check 3 cannot re-verify). Then: refuse on `blocking_warnings()` non-empty (the property-4 state), re-verify `config_digest` against the presented canonicalised config, `session_id` against `_resolve_session_identity`, and `transcript_digest` against the decrypted ON-DISK transcript. Every refusal is typed `NoteWriteRefusedError` and leaves the disk unchanged. The stale-note unlink lives INSIDE `write_transcript` (covers both transcription and recovery paths), unlink-FIRST and fail-closed: an unremovable stale note aborts the transcript write (`StoreWriteError`), because the hazard is exactly the stale (note, transcript) pairing; a crash between unlink and write loses only an already-superseded note. Tests: `TestNoteArtifactIO` (15) in `test_session_store.py` + 2 unlink tests in `test_transcription.py`.
- [x] 🟩 6.3: **A controller-owned `generating` lease**, not UI guards alone. Prefer a controller operation that performs scoped artifact access over exposing raw directory/crypto accessors. While the lease is held, block `start()`, `complete()`, `discard()`, session retirement, and recovered-key destruction. Add the lease to `SessionControllerLike` and `FakeController`.
  - The UI-guard-only design was insufficient: `start()` (`session.py:285-290`) calls `_retire_locked` (`:554`) on a **queued** session, dropping the in-memory handle a generation worker depends on — and `SessionController` documents any-thread safety, so a UI button state cannot be the guard.
  - **The lease is a TOKEN spanning the whole operation, not the worker.** Acquire before the `TaskThread` starts; release only after the GUI-thread `write_note` succeeds or failure cleanup completes. A worker-scoped lease released on callable return leaves the custody-critical gap exactly where `write_note` runs.
  - **Recovered sessions bypass `SessionController` entirely** — `main_window.py:194-220` calls store primitives directly — so controller guards alone cannot protect them. Route live AND recovered Complete / Discard / key-destruction through one lease-aware custody coordinator.
  - Done when: tests cover `start()`-, Complete-, Discard-, and `_destroy_recovered_crypto`-during-generation on both live and recovered paths, plus a barrier test firing exactly after worker return and before `write_note`.
  - **Done (2026-08-11, executor `stage-6`).** `GenerationLease` (identity-compared opaque token, no state to forge) + `GenerationInProgressError` in `session.py`; `SessionController.begin_generation()` / `end_generation(lease)` / `generating`. The lease is deliberately COARSE — one per controller, state-agnostic (a recovered generation runs with no live session at all), at most one in flight; over-blocking fails toward safety. `end_generation` is idempotent for the released token (cleanup may run twice) but REFUSES a token that is not the held one. While held, `_refuse_while_generating` (under the controller lock) blocks `start()` (checked at entry, before any side effect), `complete()`, `discard()`, and — defense in depth, the guard lives at the mechanism not only today's callers — `_retire_locked` itself. The RECOVERED path routes through three new coordinator operations that perform the scoped custody action inside the same lease check and lock: `complete_recovered(directory, crypto)` (Flow 2 ordering via `complete_session`), `discard_recovered(directory, crypto)`, `destroy_recovered_crypto(crypto)`; `main_window.py`'s recovered callbacks and `_destroy_recovered_crypto` now call ONLY these (raw `complete_session`/`discard_session` imports removed from the window). On a refused in-memory-key destruction the window RETAINS the reference so the worker's key survives and the release path re-runs the cleanup — Phase 7's busy guards own keeping view swaps unreachable during generation (recorded residue, not smoothed). All five ops added to `SessionControllerLike` and `FakeController` (fake has a configurable `generation_error`). Two DELIBERATE non-extensions, recorded in the coordinator's comment block (wording corrected in round 25 LOW-003): the 24 h expiry sweep is NOT lease-blocked — the shipped wiring already keeps it away from every session a generation could target (`app.sweep_protected_ids` covers the controller's non-terminal session incl. QUEUED, round 42 MED-001, plus the recovery checkouts, PR round 18), and if that wiring ever changed the race still fails CLOSED (key destroyed → `write_note`/Complete refuse); and `recovery.py`'s list-discard needs no lease (a checked-out generating session is excluded from the listing and list actions are disabled while any checkout exists). Done-when tests: `TestGenerationLeaseTokens` + `TestGenerationLeaseRecoveredPath` + `TestGenerationLeaseLivePath` in `test_session_machine.py` — start/Complete/Discard-during-generation on the live queued session (handle + custody proven untouched by the refusals), Complete/Discard/key-destruction-during-generation on the recovered path, the BARRIER test (worker returned, `write_note` not yet run → still refused; released only by explicit `end_generation`), and the worker-failure variant (lease held until cleanup releases) — plus 4 `main_window` routing tests in `test_ui_screens.py` including `_destroy_recovered_crypto`-during-generation retaining the reference.

      2. `_consolidated` must keep grouping across the WHOLE assertion. Consolidating PER CLAUSE would keep both and hard-block a legitimate bilateral note. Claims from every clause must reach one `_consolidated` call.

    - **Atomic.** The signature change forces both producers and the single consumer together; it cannot be landed in stages.

  - **The doc**, in the shape of `docs/testing/speaker-measurement.md` (what it decides, preparing, running, what is measured, custody), headed DRAFT — for practitioner ratification at Task 9.1, every number tagged *proposed*:
    1. *What the gate decides.* Copy-to-Cliniko enablement only, separate from 9.2's completion gate; a fail classifies 3A honestly as internal infrastructure until 3B and copy stays off.
    2. *The transcript set.* Consultations recorded LIVE through the app as ordinary sessions — the app is the instrument; no import path exists and none is built — each MOCK (the practitioner acting both roles, as at the Phase 2 gate) or consented, the practitioner choosing at ratification; retention is the app's own (destroyed at Complete or Discard), and a set kept for re-use at 3B's gate must be mock and falls under the Task 2.3 retention decision. Composition, proposed: 10 consultations (8–12) spanning new-patient and follow-up encounters; at least three body regions with one prefill seed each; every axis of `PLAN.md`'s AI-quality list that the extractive path can express, at least once (negation and changing symptoms; left/right; numbers, medications, doses and measurements; small talk; uncertain speech and a contradiction; an end-of-consultation and a new-patient greeting); one consultation whose autofill trigger is spoken by the clinician and one where only the patient speaks it; one where the clinician role must be corrected from the preselection; one three-speaker consultation (the D-S1 case, shared with Task 2.3). The practitioner ratifies the count and the mix.
    3. *The rubric*, scored per note during review in the Note tab beside the transcript, BEFORE Complete or Discard, as counts and yes/no only — never transcript or note text: R1 routing = assertions in the right canonical section / assertions; R2 coverage = clinically material spoken items present / material items (tallied from the transcript panel); R3 noise = assertions that must be deleted / assertions; R4 safety = surviving wrong-side, wrong-dose, negation-flipped or patient-speculation-in-a-clinician-owned-section items after review (expected 0); R5 accelerators = proposals confirmed / proposals offered; R6 net effort = "faster than writing from scratch?" yes/no, plus minutes to review. Pass rule, proposed: R4 = 0 on every note (one breach fails the gate outright); R6 yes on a majority; R1 ≥ 80 % and R3 ≤ 20 % over the set's totals. Thresholds are ratified BEFORE scoring starts, never after.
    4. *Procedure.* Per consultation: record → finish → transcribe → confirm role and template profile → Generate → score in the Note tab → Discard (mock) or Complete. The sheet is the Task 9.1 table in this plan: one row per consultation with the R1–R6 columns, a totals row, and the decision line (date, rubric version, set id, pass or fail).
    5. *What a decision flips.* The checklist is DERIVED at flip time by the grep in the Phase 9 prep anchors, never copied from a list; the doc names today's sites only as examples (the flag and its comment in `ui/models.py`, the `ui/note.py` module docstring, the shipped-state pin in `tests/test_ui_screens.py`, the "ships DISABLED" statements in `docs/design-system.md`, `docs/security/threat-model.md`, `docs/security/data-flow-map.md`, `PLAN.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, and this plan's Accepted Assumption and shipping-gate lines). A pass flips the flag to `True` with the decision date, set id and rubric version in its comment and reconciles every site; a fail leaves it `False` and records the dated fail at the same sites.
